#!/usr/bin/env bash
set -euo pipefail

cd "$(dirname "$0")/.."

if [[ ! -f .env ]]; then
  echo "Copy .env.example to .env and set RELAY_DB_SA_PASSWORD <redacted>" >&2
  exit 1
fi

if curl -s -o /dev/null http://localhost:5080; then
  echo "Port 5080 is already in use; stop the other API first." >&2
  exit 1
fi
if curl -s -o /dev/null http://localhost:4200; then
  echo "Port 4200 is already in use; stop the other web server first." >&2
  exit 1
fi

api_pid=""
web_pid=""
kill_tree() {
  local child
  for child in $(pgrep -P "$1" 2>/dev/null); do
    kill_tree "$child"
  done
  kill "$1" 2>/dev/null || true
}
stop_all() {
  trap '' INT TERM
  # dotnet run and npm start both launch the real server as a child, so stop the whole tree.
  for pid in $web_pid $api_pid; do
    kill_tree "$pid"
    wait "$pid" 2>/dev/null || true
  done
  echo "Database is still running. Stop it with: docker compose down (add -v to wipe the data)"
}
trap stop_all EXIT
trap 'exit 130' INT TERM

docker compose up -d --wait db

if [[ ! -f web/node_modules/.package-lock.json ]]; then
  (cd web && npm ci)
fi

dotnet run --project src/Relay.Api &
api_pid=$!

echo "Waiting for the API on http://localhost:5080 ..."
for _ in $(seq 1 120); do
  if ! kill -0 "$api_pid" 2>/dev/null; then
    echo "The API exited before it became ready." >&2
    exit 1
  fi
  if curl -fsS -o /dev/null http://localhost:5080/api/accounts 2>/dev/null; then
    break
  fi
  sleep 1
done
if ! curl -fsS -o /dev/null http://localhost:5080 …[clipped in markdown; full text in raw/]

### 🤖 ASSISTANT — 2026-09-29T00:56:54.064Z

I started the app with `scripts/dev.sh` and took Playwright screenshots of the current design. It's still running at http://localhost:4200.

**What the current design looks like:**
- **No styling at all.** It's browser defaults: native selects and buttons, system font, and no header or footer. The content is a narrow column in a lot of white space.
- **The week control is only Previous/Next.** You can't jump straight to a week, so getting from Jul 20 back to the Jun 1 spike takes 7 clicks. The disabled Next button is faint grey.
- **The filters don't hold their position.** They wrap onto two uneven rows, and the week controls disappear while loading or on error, so the layout jumps.
- **The account summary doesn't stand out.** The total, the "Compared with…" line and the status are three loose lines. A normal status has no symbol, while flagged ones get a coloured ▲ or ▼.
- **The location table is hard to scan.**
  - A flagged row is only marked by its coloured status text.
  - "Usually X–Y a week" is repeated on every row.
  - The "not enough history" row has an empty usual-range cell.
  - On account 6's spike, all 15 rows are identical red text.
- **The footnotes are a bullet list under the table.** They would fit naturally in a footer.
- **Loading, error and empty states are plain.** Loading is just "Loading…", the error is one line with a native "Try again" button, and the empty account is a single sentence.
- **Mobile at 390px feels broken.** The table squeezes each cell onto 3 lines, the Previous/Next buttons turn into big blocks, and the account title wraps.

I sent the screenshots and these points to the designer, which was already building the mockups. I asked it to show how the redesign fixes each one. Once it's done I'll publish the mockups to the design canvas and bring you the link.

No Angular changes will be made until you approve the design.

## Summary
Mockups for the DASH-247 redesign, ready to publish:
- the default view and the week picker open;
- account 6's spike week and the week after it;
- four states: not enough history, empty account 20, loading, error;
- mobile at 390px;
- a design spec with tokens, badges, picker anatomy, the library choice and the copy proposals.

All numbers are real seed values and all existing copy is verbatim from `docs/acceptance-criteria.md`. New copy is marked as a proposal.

**Visual direction.** Calm and monochrome on a warm off-white ground (`#F6F5F2`), with white cards, near-black text (`#1A1B1E`) and no brand accent colour. Colour appears only for status direction: rust `#8A3A0A` for "higher" and blue `#173F8A` for "lower", at different lightness, always with ▲/▼ and text. Type pairing: Instrument Serif for the page title and the one number that answers "is this normal?", Geist for everything else, with fixed-width numerals.

**How each point of your review is addressed**
1. **Unstyled defaults:** there is now a header, a surface-colour ground, and cards for the summary and the table. A full-width footer holds the method notes. Content is 1120px wide and centred.
2. **Week control:** a trigger button shows the approved week label (C-17) and opens an Angular Material calendar where only Mondays between `earliestWeek` and `latestCompleteWeek` can be picked. Jumping from Jul 20 to Jun 1 now takes about three actions instead of seven clicks. Previous/next buttons stay. The disabled state uses a filled background, not faint grey text.
3. **Filters:** one fixed row (Viewing as 280px · Week 576px · Activity type 196px) with labels above each control. On first load, a placeholder the same width holds the week slot, so nothing shifts. The Previous/Next buttons stay out of the DOM there, so UI-44 still holds.
4. **Summary:** heading and method line, then a large figure with the "usually X–Y a week" text beside it, and a status badge on the right. "Within usual range" now gets a decorative, screen-reader-hidden icon like the flagged statuses do. The text content of each line is unchanged.
5. **Table:**
   - Flagged rows get a light tint of their status colour, semibold location and count, and a filled badge. Normal rows are plain.
   - The words in "Usually … a week" are muted so the numbers stand out; a copy change that removes the repetition is offered as optional proposal R-01.
   - The Status column is now 30% wide.
   - Rows without enough history keep the Usual range cell empty (UI-45) and let the status text wrap.
   - In the spike week, the uniform tint across all 15 rows is the message: the whole account moved. SpikeAfter shows 2 flagged rows among 13 normal ones.
6. **Footnotes:** moved into the footer under a proposed heading, "About these numbers", in two columns.
7. **Loading, error, empty:**
   - Loading keeps "Loading…" as a status message and adds a hidden-from-screen-readers skeleton.
   - Error is a card announced as an alert, with an icon and a primary "Try again" button.
   - Empty account 20 is a centred card; the whole week control is disabled but still shows the week.
8. **Mobile:** controls stack. The trigger is full width with two half-width stepper buttons under it. Each table row becomes two lines (Location + Status, then Events + Usual range). The column headers stay in the DOM but are visually hidden, and a visual-only "Events" label comes from CSS, so cell text is unchanged.

## Changed
All files are under `/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-josemejia-Documents-Software-Development-Qualitara-relay-activity-health/74b25094-c0f6-4b4a-a9ba-66244c7b622d/scratchpad/canvas/project/`:
- `canvas.json` (10 boards in 5 titled rows, 3 sticky notes)
- `Main.dc.html` · `Picker.dc.html` · `Spike.dc.html` · `SpikeAfter.dc.html` · `InsufficientData.dc.html` · `EmptyAccount.dc.html` · `Loading.dc.html` · `Error.dc.html` · `Mobile.dc.html` · `Spec.dc.html`

I also wrote a throwaway checking script, `…/scratchpad/check_tags.py`; it is not part of the deliverable.

## Verification
- `python3 -m json.tool …/project/canvas.json` → `JSON_OK`
- `python3 …/scratchpad/check_tags.py …/project` → every file reports `problems: [] unclosed: [] unquoted: 0 size_ok: True support.js: True`
- Contrast ratios were computed with the WCAG luminance formula. The key ones:

|---|---|
| ink / white | 17.22:1 |
| secondary text / white | 7.25:1 |
| lightest text / white | 5.19:1 (4.76 on the page ground) |

## Deviations
- **Extra board:** `SpikeAfter.dc.html` (account 6, week of Jun 8) wasn't requested. Jun 1 has all 15 rows flagged, so this board is the one that shows flagged rows standing out among normal ones.
- **Row gaps:** rows are 420px apart instead of 120px, to fit the row titles that must sit at least 223px above each row.
- **Two status colours instead of one accent:** per your note to keep the rust/blue semantics. The UI chrome itself has no accent colour.

## Uncertain
**Copy proposals for product** (none approved yet):
- **H-01** header: "Relay" · "Customer admin". Plain text, not a link, so it can't reset the URL.
- **F-01** footer heading: "About these numbers".
- **F-02** footer base line: "Relay · Activity health". It also shows while loading and on error.
- **W-01** visible "Week" label over the week control. It is already the group's accessible label today.
- **W-02** trigger accessible name "Choose week, {C-17}"; the calendar dialog's name is "Choose week".
- **W-03** "Weeks run Monday to Sunday."
- **W-04** "Weeks from Mon Jan 26 to Mon Jul 20, 2026", built from the response's week bounds.
- **W-05** Material's built-in labels "Previous month" / "Next month" / "Choose month and year": accept or override.
- **T-01** table caption "Locations — most unusual first".
- **R-01** (optional, not drawn): cell "3–12" with header "Usual range (a week)". This changes C-05/C-19.

**Library choice:** Angular Material, confirmed.
- Component: `MatCalendar` (`&lt;mat-calendar&gt;`) inside a CDK connected overlay with focus trapping, opened by our own trigger.
- Bindings: `minDate` / `maxDate` = the week bounds; `dateFilter` = Monday within the bounds; `dateClass` shades Tue–Sun of the selected week; `selectedChange` emits the existing `weekSelected`.
- Adapter: `provideDateFnsAdapter()` with the date-fns `enUS` locale set to `weekStartsOn: 1`.
- Packages to add: `@angular/material`, `@angular/cdk`, `@angular/material-date-fns-adapter`, `date-fns`. That's a `package.json` change, which `frontend` owns.
- The spec lists the rejected alternatives. The selects stay native.

**Behaviour and contract questions:**
- **No API or contract change needed.** `DashboardFilters` already has the week inputs and the `weekSelected` output. Moving the footnote from inside `&lt;main&gt;` into `&lt;footer&gt;` and adding the caption are template-only changes, but should be checked against UI-06 and the table tests.
- **Calendar quirk:** Material's month view leaves days from the neighbouring months blank, so the week starting Mon Jun 29 is only pickable from the June view.
- **Spike values:** only Site C (67, usually 1–7) and "all 15 higher" are PLAN goldens for Jun 1. The other 14 rows come from your screenshot of the running app's API output. The Jun 8 rows come from `analysis/goldens/promoted_goldens_out.md`.
- **Error board assumption:** it assumes the accounts list loaded. If that call fails too, "Viewing as" is empty, which is existing accepted behaviour.
- **Mobile picker:** the bottom-sheet version under 640px is specified but not drawn.

- The coordinator publishes `project/canvas.json` first, then the ten `.dc.html` files, and brings the canvas to the user.
- After approval: product finalises the copy proposals, the architect confirms the no-contract-change reading, and frontend adds the Material packages and implements from `Spec.dc.html`. **Look and feel:** calm and nearly monochrome, on a warm off-white background with white cards. The page title and the headline number use Instrument Serif; everything else uses Geist. Colour only marks direction: rust with ▲ for "Higher than usual", blue with ▼ for "Lower than usual", always with the text. "Within usual range" now gets its own symbol too.

- The filters sit in one row that no longer jumps while loading.
- Flagged rows are tinted and bold.

<result>## Summary
I made the three requested changes. Instrument Serif is gone: the page title and the headline number now use Geist at weight 600 with tighter letter spacing. The mobile board and everything about it are removed. Nothing was added for pagination. Layout, colours, copy and numbers on the other boards are unchanged. Nothing was rendered, so I haven't seen how any of this looks.

## Changed
All files are under `/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-josemejia-Documents-Software-Development-Qualitara-relay-activity-health/74b25094-c0f6-4b4a-a9ba-66244c7b622d/scratchpad/canvas/project/`
- **Font swap on 8 artboards:** `Main.dc.html`, `Picker.dc.html`, `Spike.dc.html`, `SpikeAfter.dc.html` and `InsufficientData.dc.html` (title and headline number), plus `EmptyAccount.dc.html`, `Loading.dc.html` and `Error.dc.html` (title only; they have no number).
  - The Google Fonts `&lt;link&gt;` now loads only `family=Geist:wght@400;500;600&amp;amp;display=swap`.
  - Title: `font-weight: 600; font-size: 48px; line-height: 52px; letter-spacing: -0.025em;`, taking Geist from the root.
  - Headline number: `font-weight: 600; font-size: 72px; line-height: 72px; letter-spacing: -0.035em;`, still with `tabular-nums`.
- **`Spec.dc.html`:**
  - Same `&lt;link&gt;` change. The spec title is now Geist 600 at 56/60.
  - The "Two faces" bullet is now "One family", describing the new setup.
  - Type scale: the title and number rows now show Geist 600 with their sizes and letter spacing. All four "mobile" size notes are removed, and the fallbacks line no longer mentions Instrument Serif.
  - §8 "Responsive rules" is now "8 · Desktop layout". It keeps only the old ≥ 1200 row (filter widths, table column widths, footnote columns), relabelled "Filters and table". The 640–1199, &lt; 640 and bottom-sheet picker rows are deleted.
  - The frame height and `$preview` stay at 4300. §8 sits next to the taller §7 in the same row, so removing rows shouldn't shorten the board.
- **`Mobile.dc.html`:** deleted.
- **`canvas.json`: changed.**
  - Removed the `Mobile.dc.html` board and its `order` entry, the `row-mobile` title and the `sticky-mobile` note.
  - Moved the Spec board and its title up into the freed slot (board y 7380 → 5460, title y 7080 → 5160). This keeps the usual 120px gap between rows instead of leaving an empty 2000px band.

## Verification
- `python3 -c "import json; …"`: canvas.json parses. `boards` and `order` list the same 9 boards with Mobile gone. The notes are `row-default, row-spike, row-states, row-spec, sticky-picker-keys, sticky-spike-source`. Spec is at y 5460, h 4300.
- `grep -c -i "instrument\|serif'\|georgia" *.html`: 0 in every file.
- `grep -n -i "mobile\|bottom sheet\|responsive\|&amp;lt; 640\|640–" *.html canvas.json`: no matches.
- `grep -c "support.js" *.html`: 1 in every artboard, so the script tag is intact.

## Deviations
- I moved the Spec board and its title up in `canvas.json`. This goes slightly beyond "re-position notes only"; without it there would be a large empty gap where the mobile row was.
- The spec's desktop filter and table measurements were in the responsive section. I kept them under a new "Desktop layout" heading rather than delete desktop content along with the responsive rules.

## Uncertain
- **Font choice: Geist 600** for the page title and headline number. Reason: one family with tight letter spacing looks confident and corporate rather than editorial. It needs no second font to load, and its figures are lining with tabular spacing available, so numbers never shift width.
- The heavier title and number are wider than the old serif, so text may wrap differently. The widest cases (the 880 and 102 numbers, and the 56px spec title in its half-width column) are the ones to check on the canvas.
- No copy changes for `product`: only the spec's own descriptive text was touched.
- The spec still mentions a 44×44 minimum size for controls. I kept it because it's an accessibility rule, not a mobile one.

## Next
- `frontend`: drop any Instrument Serif or other second font from the app, and apply the Geist 600 styles above to the h1 and the summary number. Don't build breakpoints, the stacked mobile table or the bottom-sheet picker, and don't add pagination.
- Coordinator: the canvas hasn't been rendered. Look at the Main, Spike, SpikeAfter and Spec boards to confirm the title and number look right.</result>

## Changed

**Copy decisions (new table §0.1b)**

| Id | Designer id | Verdict | Final string |
|---|---|---|---|
| C-24 | H-01 | Accepted | `Relay` · `Customer admin`. Plain text: not a link, not a heading |
| C-25 | F-01 | Accepted | `About these numbers` |

| C-31 | W-05 | Accepted (Material's defaults) | `Previous month`, `Next month`, `Choose month and year` |
| C-32 | T-01 | Accepted | `Locations — most unusual first` |

**New checks (§4.4)**
- **UI-46:** header text appears in every state, clicking it doesn't change the URL, and UI-43 (one `&lt;h1&gt;`) still holds.
- **UI-47:** footer base line appears in every state, and the footnote lines sit under the new footer heading.
- **UI-48:** the picker only allows Mondays from earliestWeek to latestCompleteWeek. It uses account 14 (Jan 26 to Jul 20, not Jul 27, not Jan 19) and account 8 (from Feb 2). Choosing a week adds a history entry, and the helper text is checked.
- **UI-49:** keyboard use: accessible names, Enter to open, ↑ to move one week, Enter to pick, Escape to close, focus returns to the trigger.

**Amended entries (each marked "amended by Redesign 2026-09-29")**
- **C-07 and UI-20:** the whole week control is disabled for account 20, including the picker trigger, but still shows the week.
- **UI-03:** adds the table caption.
- **UI-05:** "not by colour alone" now covers row tints, filled badges and the decorative icon on "Within usual range", checked against UI-10 and UI-18. I extended this entry instead of adding a duplicate.

- **UI-44:** the week control is not on the page until the first report loads, and a same-width placeholder holds its place.

## Verification

## Uncertain
Copy decisions the user should see:
1. **W-02 reworded.** The proposal was `Choose week, {C-17}`, but the design spec itself says the name should *start* with the visible text. I changed it to `Mon Jul 20 – Sun Jul 26, 2026, choose week` so it follows the spec and WCAG's recommended practice. It reads a little less naturally. The designer's order also passes WCAG, if the user prefers it.
2. **T-01 "most unusual first".** The table also lists normal and not-enough-history rows below the flagged ones. The phrase is still honest about the order, and it answers "which location needs attention" in the same "usual" wording the status labels use. An alternative is "needing attention first".

It needs only the inputs that already exist. PLAN doesn't ban UI libraries, and the prefer-libraries rule supports this. `package.json` belongs to `frontend`. The new contract is `WeekPicker` (see task 2). |
| Header / footer | **Valid, with a condition.** UI-43 requires "exactly one `&lt;h1&gt;` … `Activity health`" in every state, so the "Relay" wordmark must not be an `&lt;h1&gt;`. |

The footer must be a sibling of `&lt;main&gt;` inside `DashboardPage`'s template, not in the `App` shell. Otherwise it can't read the report, and the UI-06/13/19/20b/22 page tests won't see it. |

| Loading / error / empty treatments | **Valid.** C-20, C-21 and C-07 stay word for word. UI-20 "both week buttons are disabled" still holds, and the trigger is disabled the same way (`earliestWeek == latestCompleteWeek`). The skeleton must contain no text, because UI-04 scans the rendered text. |

| Desktop only | **Valid.** PLAN has no responsive requirement. |
| No pagination | **Valid.** §5.2 returns every location (at most 15 in the seed). |

```ts
export class WeekPicker {  // selector app-week-picker
  readonly week = input.required&lt;WeekRange&gt;();
  readonly earliestWeek = input.required&lt;string&gt;();
  readonly latestCompleteWeek = input.required&lt;string&gt;();
  readonly weekSelected = output&lt;string&gt;();
}
```
Its disabled state is derived, not an input. `DashboardFilters` renders it and forwards `weekSelected` to its own output of the same name.

<result>## Summary
Angular Material, CDK, the date-fns adapter, date-fns and self-hosted Geist are installed and wired up, so the WeekPicker red suite can run against the real `MatCalendar`. The build passes with no warnings and `npm test` still shows 124 passing. I did not touch any component, template or test. Committed as `eac3f4f` in this worktree.

## Changed
- `/Users/josemejia/Documents/Software Development/Qualitara/relay-activity-health/.claude/worktrees/agent-a5d8c1b7220804d3a/web/package.json` and `web/package-lock.json` (updated by `npm install`). Exact installed versions:
  - `@angular/material` 22.2.0 (`^22.2.0`)
  - `@angular/cdk` 22.2.0 (`^22.2.0`)
  - `@angular/material-date-fns-adapter` 22.2.0 (`^22.2.0`)
  - `date-fns` 4.4.0 (`^4.4.0`)
  - `@fontsource/geist` 5.3.0 (`^5.3.0`)
  - `@angular/forms` 22.2.0 (`^22.2.0`), see Deviations
  - Angular core is 22.2.0.
- `web/angular.json`: the global style changed from `src/styles.css` to `src/styles.scss`, and `@fontsource/geist/400.css`, `500.css` and `600.css` come before it in `styles`.
- `web/src/app/app.config.ts`: adds `provideDateFnsAdapter()` and `{ provide: MAT_DATE_LOCALE, useValue: { ...enUS, options: { ...enUS.options, weekStartsOn: 1 as const } } }`.
- `web/src/styles.css` was renamed to `web/src/styles.scss` (it was empty). It now contains only `@use '@angular/material' as mat;` and `html { color-scheme: light; @include mat.theme((color: mat.$azure-palette, typography: 'Geist', density: 0)); }`.

## Verification

## Deviations
- **`@angular/forms` added explicitly.** Material and CDK 22 require it as a peer dependency. npm had already installed it silently, so I listed it in `package.json` to make the dependency visible.
- **Global styles moved from CSS to SCSS.** `mat.theme` and the `mat.datepicker-overrides` that the Spec asks for are Sass-only, so the global file had to become `.scss`. Components still use CSS.
- **Fonts load from `angular.json`, not from inside the Sass file.** Pulling the `.css` files in with Sass `@use` fails because `"400"` is not a valid Sass namespace.
- **Spec vs. PLAN on fonts.** The Spec says "One Google Fonts request". I followed the task and PLAN's prefer-libraries rule and self-hosted Geist through @fontsource instead.

## Uncertain
- The existing `body{font-family:'Geist',...}` rule was in `Spec.dc.html`, not in `styles.css`, so I added no body styles. `mat.theme` sets typography tokens only, so the page's own font stack and background still need to be added in the implementation step.
- `mat.$azure-palette` is a stand-in for a "minimal theme". The Spec's tokens should replace it through `mat.datepicker-overrides` later.
- The adapter and locale are registered only in `appConfig`. WeekPicker specs that use TestBed need to provide `provideDateFnsAdapter()` and `MAT_DATE_LOCALE` themselves.

## Next
- test-author: `MatCalendar`, `@angular/cdk/overlay` and the date-fns adapter can all be imported now. Use `parseISO` for local calendar dates.
- frontend (implementation step): add the design tokens and body typography to `src/styles.scss`. That file now accepts Sass, so `mat.datepicker-overrides` can go there.
- Coordinator: merge `eac3f4f` from this worktree's branch.</result>

**Checking the tests themselves:** I copied the picker spec into a temporary spec file with a throwaway `WeekPicker` built on `mat-calendar` and `cdkConnectedOverlay`, and all 15 tests passed. That shows the harness, overlay, keyboard and focus mechanics work in jsdom. I then deleted the file; it was never committed. The expected values all come from the acceptance criteria.

## Deviations
None from PLAN or CLAUDE.md. Two consequences you should know about:
- 7 new tests already pass: the UI-05 tests (4 in LocationTable, 3 in AccountSummary). The current markup has no icons and exactly one status text, so they meet the rule today. They will catch an icon that adds visible text or is read aloud, or a status that is announced twice.
- I extended 3 existing UI-44 tests (the one in filters and the two recovery tests on the page) to check the picker as well, so they are red now. This is intended, because the redesign amends UI-44.

## Uncertain
1. **Opening with Enter:** jsdom does not turn a synthetic Enter keypress into a button click. A test that sends Enter would push the implementer to add a `keydown.enter` handler, and in a real browser that would open and then immediately close the picker. So the test instead checks that the trigger is a native `&lt;button type="button"&gt;` that Tab can reach, and opens it with a click. The ↑/↓, Enter-to-pick, Enter on a non-Monday and Escape steps are all tested with real key presses.
2. **Trigger name:** the mockups say `Choose week, {C-17}` (the older W-02 wording). I followed the acceptance criteria's reworded C-28, `{C-17}, choose week`.
3. **Shading of Tue–Sun:** the class name isn't specified anywhere. The test only requires some CSS class that all six days Jul 21–26 share and that no day outside that week has.
4. **Account 8's lower bound:** blocking earlier weeks by a minimum date or by a date filter both pass. January is either unreachable or has nothing selectable.
5. **UI-44 placeholder:** a same-width placeholder can't be measured in jsdom. I test only what the Loading mockup shows: the "Week" label is there, and the stepper and picker are not.
6. **Header, footer and caption markup:** the tests look for `header`/`[role=banner]` and `footer`/`[role=contentinfo]` outside `&lt;main&gt;`, matching the Main mockup. For the table they accept a `&lt;caption&gt;`, `aria-label` or `aria-labelledby`.
7. **Waiting for focus:** Material moves focus after a render and a `setTimeout`, so the picker tests wait a few event-loop turns before checking focus.
8. **C-30 across years:** I derived `Weeks from Mon Dec 29, 2025 to Mon Jul 20, 2026` from the rule "if the two years differ, both dates show their year". 2025-12-29 is a Monday.

## Next
- `frontend`: implement `WeekPicker` as PLAN §13 and the design spec describe. Keep the trigger inside `app-week-picker`, give the dialog `role="dialog"`, and move focus to the selected day when the picker opens. Render it from `DashboardFilters` behind a visible "Week" label. Move the footer and add the header outside `&lt;main&gt;` in `DashboardPage`, and add the caption to `LocationTable`.
- Any test harness for the app needs `provideDateFnsAdapter()` and the Monday-first `enUS` locale, as the specs now do.

  - filters: `labels the week control "Week", visibly and as the group accessible name`
- **C-27:** filters `labels the week control "Week"…`

- **C-29 and C-30:** week-picker `shows "Weeks run Monday to Sunday." and "$rangeHelper" for $account` (account 14, account 8, and bounds in different years)

## Deviations
Where the build still differs from the mockups:
1. **Weekday headers read M T W T F S S, not Mon Tue ….** That is the Material calendar's default. Changing it needs a small custom date adapter in `app.config.ts`, which replaces the approved `provideDateFnsAdapter()` setup, so I left it.
2. **The picker header reads "Jul 2026", not "July 2026".** That is the adapter's default month format, and Material forces it to capitals. I turned it back into "Jul 2026" with CSS. The full month name would need a date-format override in `app.config.ts`.
3. **The month caret and navigation buttons are Material's own:** a small ▾ triangle and Material's arrow buttons and hover effect, rather than the drawn chevrons.
4. **The week group is 576px wide, per Spec §8.** The stepper and trigger inside it are about 561px, so the gap before "Activity type" is about 15px wider than in the mockup. This keeps the loaded control and the loading placeholder the same width, so nothing shifts.
5. **Material still gets the azure palette underneath.** Every colour you can see is overridden with the Spec colours, but the palette stays so Material's internal colour settings are all defined.
6. **The dashboard route now loads on demand.** Adding Material pushed the first download to 567.71 kB, over the 500 kB warning limit. I did this instead of raising the limit in `angular.json`.
7. **The skeleton pulses (opacity) rather than sweeping.** It uses the Spec's 1.2s timing and stops under reduced motion.

## Uncertain
- On a load error I kept the footer to the base line only, even when an earlier report exists. That matches the Error mockup and the current behaviour.
- Choosing the week that is already selected closes the picker without sending `weekSelected`, so no navigation happens.
- The Spec's inset focus ring on calendar days is added with CSS on keyboard focus (`:focus-visible`). The screenshot shows it on Jul 13.

## Next
- The coordinator should merge `3b12feb`. If you want Deviations 1 and 2 matched exactly, the architect needs to decide on the `app.config.ts` change first.
- For the e2e specs: the trigger's name ends with ", choose week", the dialog is `role="dialog"` named "Choose week", and the Material calendar test helpers work inside it.
- Playwright is only installed in the e2e worktree (`agent-af1bc63b76d53c82b/web/node_modules/playwright`), not in this `web/`.</result>

<result>## Verdict: CHANGES REQUIRED (minor only, no blockers)

I ran `npm ci`, `npm test -- --watch=false` and `npm run build` in my worktree, detached at 3b12feb.
- **Tests:** 165 of 165 pass.
- **Build:** clean, with no budget warning.
- **Test files:** none were edited by the implementer, and no `*.spec.ts` appears in the diff.

## Findings (most severe first)

- **[Major] `web/src/app/features/dashboard/components/week-picker.ts:154-163` — picking the currently selected week never closes the picker.**
  - Material's `_dateSelected` emits `selectedChange` only when the date differs from the selected one (`datepicker.mjs:1018`). The `!== this.week().start` guard in `choose()` is therefore unreachable.
  - Failing input: open the picker with Mon Jul 20 selected. Focus lands on Jul 20 (UI-49). Press Enter or click Jul 20. Nothing happens: the dialog stays open and focus does not return to the trigger.
  - This breaks "whenever the picker closes, focus returns to the trigger" (UI-49) and expected behaviour. Escape still works.
  - Fix: also handle the calendar's `_userSelection` output (or `(yearSelected)`/click) and call `close()`. Keep emitting `weekSelected` only when the week changed. Add a test for the re-select case. That test needs the user's approval first, because the red suite is frozen.

- **[Minor] `web/src/styles.scss:71-90` — CSS coupled to Material's internal structure.**
  - Selectors depend on `.mdc-button__label &gt; span`, `tr:has(&gt; .mat-calendar-body-label[colspan='7'])`, `.mat-calendar-body-cell-container:nth-child(7)` and `:has(.mat-calendar-body-selected)`.
  - The installed Material is `22.2.0-next.7`, a prerelease, so these can silently break on the next bump. Nothing in the suite would catch it, because the tests do not render Material's real layout.
  - Fix: pin the version, or have the e2e layer cover the picker's appearance.

- **[Minor] Duplicated colour and size literals.**
  - `styles.scss:35-60` repeats the token hex values (`#1a1b1e` nine times) instead of `var(--color-ink)`. The `mat.*-overrides` mixins accept `var()`.
  - `week-picker.ts:90` uses `#b9bbbf`, which is not a spec token.
  - Trigger width 300, dialog width 350 and field widths 280/576/196 are magic numbers (CLAUDE rule 3).
  - Fix: reference the CSS variables.

- **[Minor] `web/src/app/features/dashboard/health-copy.ts:22-24` — `activityCount` is now dead in production code.** Only `health-copy.spec.ts` still uses it. It cannot be removed without touching the frozen tests, so leave it and note it.

- **[Minor] `week-picker.ts:25` — module-level mutable `nextDialogId`.** It is static state. Acceptable for a unique DOM id. Nit.

- **[Minor] `dashboard-filters.ts:66` vs `:30-39` — placeholder and real control differ in width.**
  - The real control is 561px wide (464→1025 in the screenshot). The placeholder fills the whole 576px field (464→1040).
  - Loading to loaded therefore shifts the control's right edge by 15px. The Activity type field does not move, since the field is fixed at 576px, but the gap to it changes by the same 15px.
  - This is deviation 4. See the ruling below.

## Architect conditions and acceptance criteria

- **Exactly one `&lt;h1&gt;`:** met. `dashboard.page.ts:23`.
- **Wordmark not a heading:** met. It is a `&lt;span&gt;`, plain text (C-24), and neither wordmark nor area label is a link.
- **Footer placement:** met. `&lt;footer&gt;` is a sibling of `&lt;main&gt;` inside `DashboardPage`, so the page has both the banner and contentinfo landmarks. C-25 heading and C-26 base line are present. The base line also shows in loading and error.
- **Footnotes:** C-09…C-14 wording is unchanged and sits under C-25.
  - C-14 is gated on `eventType !== 'all'`.
  - The "Data as of" line is gated on `dataAsOfLabel()`.
  - The footnotes are hidden on error and while loading. That matches UI-47.
- **Icons:** `Icon` sets `host: aria-hidden="true"`. The SVGs have no `&lt;title&gt;` and `focusable=false`.
- **Loading:** C-21 sits in `role=status`. The skeleton and the table skeleton are `aria-hidden` and contain no text.
- **Week placeholder:** the elements are `aria-hidden` spans, with no C-17 or C-18 text and no buttons.
- **Empty account:** the C-07 card replaces both summary and table. The week control is disabled by derivation (`earliestWeek === latestCompleteWeek`), and the stepper buttons are disabled by `canGo*`. The footnotes stay (UI-20b).
- **"Usually X–Y a week":** the table splits it across spans, but the text content is identical. The summary reads "usually 18–38 a week", as before.
- **C-27, C-28, C-29, C-30:** met. The trigger `aria-label` is `{C-17}, choose week`, the dialog `aria-label` is `Choose week`, and the helper lines are correct with the year shown once.
- **Tokens:** every colour token in `styles.scss:4-25` matches Spec, including higher = `#A8201A`/`#FDE4E2`/`#FEF4F3`/`#F4DEDB`. Below = `#173F8A`/`#C9D8F2`/`#F3F6FC`. The popover shadow matches. The focus ring is 2px ink with a 2px offset.
- **C-31 and C-32:** Material's built-in navigation labels are kept. The caption matches C-32.
- **URL and behaviour:** `selectWeek` in `DashboardState` is unchanged and the row order is the payload order. No client-side sorting or statistics were added. The `WeekPicker` inputs and outputs are unchanged.
- **Code rules:** no comment blocks or HTML comments found. No new behaviour class needed an interface. `Icon` and `WeekPicker` are presentational.
- **Accessibility:**
  - Status is never colour-only: badges carry ▲/▼ and text, and normal/insufficient rows carry text plus a decorative icon.
  - Selectable Mondays are distinguished by fill and weight 600, not only colour.
  - Focus returns to the trigger on Escape (overlay `detach` → `close()`), on outside click and on a changed selection.
  - Contrast matches the Spec's stated ratios. The disabled-state greys are exempt.
  - `prefers-reduced-motion` disables both the popover animation and the skeleton.

## Ruling on the self-reported deviations

1. **Weekday headers "M T W T F S S" — acceptable, but a design call.**
   - The mockup and Spec §6 show "Mon Tue …". Material hard-codes `getDayOfWeekNames('narrow')` for the visible glyph (`datepicker.mjs:1164`) and adds a visually-hidden long name in the same `&lt;th&gt;`. Screen readers therefore hear "Monday".
   - The "T T" and "S S" ambiguity is visual only. It could only be changed with a custom adapter or CSS hack.
   - **Needs the designer or user to accept it.**
2. **"Jul 2026" month label — not a real problem, but trivially fixable.**
   - Both the Spec and Picker mockups say "July 2026". The date-fns adapter's `monthYearLabel` is `'LLL uuuu'`, and Material upper-cases the text (`datepicker.mjs:2076`). The CSS lowercase/first-letter hack is what produces "Jul 2026".
   - Overriding `MAT_DATE_FORMATS.display.monthYearLabel` to `'LLLL yyyy'` would give "July 2026" and let the hack stay.
   - **Design call.** Only accept it if the user prefers not to touch the format provider.
3. **Material's caret and navigation buttons — acceptable.** Spec §6 says "Material's own labels", and C-31 keeps the defaults. The nav buttons are 40px against the Spec's 44px target, which is a nit.
4. **576px week group with about 15px wider gap — acceptable visually; minor.**
   - The real control is 561px (trigger fixed at 300px, stepper buttons content-sized). The extra gap is 15px, as measured from the screenshots.
   - Cleaner fix: give both stepper buttons a fixed width so the control is exactly 576px and matches the placeholder. Alternatively size the placeholder to the control.
   - **Design call** on whether 15px matters.
5. **Azure palette underneath the overrides — acceptable, with residual risk.**
   - Every visible calendar token I traced is overridden: selected, hover, focus, today outline, header, period and navigation icons. I saw no blue in the screenshots.
   - Any un-overridden Material token (ripple, state layers in the year view) would show azure and contradict Spec's "no brand accent". I did not exercise the multi-year view. Worth a quick manual check.
6. **Lazy-loaded dashboard route — acceptable, but it goes beyond PLAN and the redesign entry.**
   - Without it, the initial bundle would have been about 570 kB. That is above the 500 kB warning threshold and below the 1 MB error threshold.
   - Result: 364.6 kB initial plus a 205.8 kB lazy chunk. The cost is an extra request waterfall for a one-route app.
   - The alternative was raising the warning budget in `angular.json`. It touches the same owner (`frontend`), so it is a one-line choice.
   - **Product call:** which route to take, and whether to record it in §13.
7. **Pulsing skeleton — acceptable.**
   - The Spec says "skeleton shimmer at 1.2s, off under reduced-motion". The implementation is a 1.2s opacity pulse with reduced-motion disabled.
   - It differs from a true shimmer but is within intent.

## Screenshots against mockups

- **Match:** the default view, spike, insufficient-data, loading, error and empty-account shots match the mockups, including the footer grid (identical CSS to `Main.dc.html`), the badges and the tints.
- **Differences:** the picker shows "Jul 2026" and "M T W T…" (items 1 and 2).

I added one red test to `week-picker.spec.ts` for re-selecting the current week. With Jul 20–26 selected, the test opens the picker and presses Enter on the focused Mon Jul 20. It expects the dialog to close, focus to return to the trigger, and `weekSelected` not to fire. In the run, this new test is the only failure and it fails because the dialog is still open; the other 165 pass. Committed as **5f855e4** on `redesign-reselect-test` (branched from 3b12feb).

## Changed
- `/Users/josemejia/Documents/Software Development/Qualitara/relay-activity-health/.claude/worktrees/agent-a2fcf7b406e39ac70/web/src/app/features/dashboard/components/week-picker.spec.ts`: new test `Enter on the already selected Mon Jul 20 closes the dialog, returns focus to the trigger and does not emit weekSelected` under `describe('keyboard (UI-49)')`. It uses the helpers already in the file and I changed nothing else.

## Verification
```
git checkout -b redesign-reselect-test 3b12feb   -&gt; Switched to a new branch
cd web &amp;&amp; npm ci                                 -&gt; found 0 vulnerabilities
npm test -- --watch=false
  × Enter on the already selected Mon Jul 20 closes the dialog, returns focus to the trigger and does not emit weekSelected
  FAIL src/app/features/dashboard/components/week-picker.spec.ts &gt; WeekPicker &gt; keyboard (UI-49) &gt; Enter on the already selected Mon Jul 20 ...

  Test Files  1 failed | 10 passed (11)
       Tests  1 failed | 165 passed (166)
git log --oneline -1 -&gt; 5f855e4 Add red test: re-selecting the current week closes the week picker
```
The test first checks that the focused cell is `20`, and that check passes. The failing assertion is `openDialogs()` being empty: the dialog is still open. Material's `selectedChange` doesn't fire when the chosen date equals the current one, so `choose()` never runs.

## Deviations
None.

## Uncertain
- The test covers Enter only, not mouse clicks. The reviewer said both paths go through the same `selectedChange` gap, so one fix should cover both. The existing "choosing Mon Jul 13" test already covers the click path for a different week.

## Next
- Frontend: when the user re-selects the current week, close the picker. Material only emits `selectedChange` when the date changes, so this needs another hook, for example `(_userSelection)` on `mat-calendar` or a keydown/click handler on the active cell. After closing, focus the trigger through the existing `close()` and don't emit.
- Traceability: UI-49 ("Whenever the picker closes, focus returns to the trigger") and UI-48/UI-49 (choosing the current week is not a navigation) are covered by the new test.

## Proposed additions
- Possibly a matching click test (`calendar.selectCell({ text: '20' })`) if the coordinator wants mouse re-selection pinned separately. I didn't add it because the task was one test.</result>

<result>## Summary
All six reviewer findings are fixed on `redesign-fixes`, commit **378b7fe** (parent 5f855e4). Tests pass 166/166 and the build is clean. I checked the result in a real browser: the header reads "July 2026", the loaded week control is exactly 576px, and re-selecting the current week closes the picker. Two things didn't go as the brief expected. For item 2, the lowercase CSS rule is still there because Material upper-cases the label in code. For item 4, the installed versions were already stable, so I pinned them without changing any version. Both are explained under Deviations.

## Changed
- `/Users/josemejia/Documents/Software Development/Qualitara/relay-activity-health/.claude/worktrees/agent-a7a65d630538d4cc7/web/src/app/features/dashboard/components/week-picker.ts`: the calendar now listens to `(_userSelection)="choose($event.value)"` instead of `selectedChange`. `_userSelection` fires on every user pick, including the date that's already selected, so the existing `choose()` closes the picker, returns focus to the trigger and emits only when the week changes. The fixed trigger width now uses `var(--week-picker-trigger-width)` and `#b9bbbf` is now `var(--color-disabled-chevron)`.
- `.../web/src/app/app.config.ts`: `provideDateFnsAdapter({...MAT_DATE_FNS_FORMATS, display: {...display, monthYearLabel: 'LLLL yyyy'}})`. This provides `MAT_DATE_FORMATS` and keeps the adapter's other formats.
- `.../web/src/app/features/dashboard/components/dashboard-filters.ts`: the field widths use custom properties. The stepper buttons get `box-sizing: border-box; flex: none` and fixed widths.
- `.../web/src/styles.scss`: new custom properties `--color-disabled-chevron: #b9bbbf` (Spec value for the disabled chevron), `--account-field-width 280`, `--week-field-width 576`, `--type-field-width 196`, `--week-picker-trigger-width 300`, `--week-step-previous-width 150`, `--week-step-next-width 128` and `--week-picker-dialog-width 350`. Every hex value in `mat.theme-overrides` and `mat.datepicker-overrides` is now `var(--color-…)`. The `.mat-calendar-body-label { visibility: hidden }` rule is replaced by the supported token `calendar-body-label-text-color: transparent`.
- `.../web/package.json`, `.../web/package-lock.json`: `@angular/material`, `@angular/cdk` and `@angular/material-date-fns-adapter` pinned to exactly `22.2.0`.

**Final package versions:** @angular/material 22.2.0, @angular/cdk 22.2.0, @angular/material-date-fns-adapter 22.2.0, @angular/core 22.2.0. `npm view` shows dist-tag latest = 22.2.0 and next = 22.2.0-rc.0.

## Verification
- `npm test -- --watch=false` gives `Test Files 11 passed (11) · Tests 166 passed (166)`, including the new re-select test.
- `npm run build` gives `Application bundle generation complete.` with no warnings.
- I served the app with `ng serve --port 4300` against the API on 5080 and drove it with Playwright from `agent-af1bc63b76d53c82b/web/node_modules`. Script: `.../scratchpad/verify.cjs`. For the loading state, the activity-health request was held back.

Manual checks in the browser:

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| Placeholder week field | 576px wide, field right edge at 1040, Activity type field at x=1064 |
| Loaded week control | 576px wide, scrollWidth 576, right edge at 1040, Activity type field at x=1064. Nothing moves between loading and loaded |
| Stepper text fits | natural widths are 144.1px ("◀ Previous week") and 118.7px ("Next week ▶"), set to 150 and 128, no overflow |
| Month label | `period label: July 2026` |
| Enter on the selected Mon Jul 20 | dialog closes, focus is back on the trigger, URL unchanged (no emit) |
| Mouse click on the selected Mon Jul 20 | same: dialog closes, focus on the trigger, URL unchanged |
| Click on Mon Jul 13 (a real change) | dialog closes, URL becomes `week=2026-07-13` |

- Screenshots are in `/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-josemejia-Documents-Software-Development-Qualitara-relay-activity-health/74b25094-c0f6-4b4a-a9ba-66244c7b622d/scratchpad/implemented/`: `picker-open-v2.png`, `account-14-2026-07-20-v2.png` and `loading-v2.png`.
- The server is stopped (`lsof -ti tcp:4300` returns nothing).

## Deviations
- **Item 2: the lowercase CSS rule is still in place.** Material 22.2.0 upper-cases the label itself in `MatCalendarHeader`: `adapter.format(activeDate, monthYearLabel).toLocaleUpperCase()` (`@angular/material/fesm2022/datepicker.mjs:2076`). With the new format alone, the header would read "JULY 2026". The format override is done, and I kept the two lowercase/first-letter rules with a one-line comment explaining why, so the header reads "July 2026". The only other way to get this is a custom `headerComponent`, which is much more code and changes the DOM the frozen harness tests rely on. If the user prefers that, it's a follow-up.
- **Item 4: there was no prerelease to remove.** On 5f855e4 the lockfile and `npm ls` already showed stable 22.2.0 for all three packages, and `^22.2.0` never matches `-next` builds. The 22.2.0-next.7 the reviewer saw likely came from a different install. I pinned the exact versions anyway, which makes that impossible in future.

## Uncertain
- `_userSelection` starts with an underscore, but it is a declared output in MatCalendar's public `.d.ts`, its event type `MatCalendarUserEvent` is exported publicly, and Material's own `MatDatepickerContent` uses it. It's the only event that fires when the already-selected date is picked again. A future Material release could rename it, and the new test would catch that.
- The 150/128 split for the stepper buttons is my choice, based on the measured text widths. Spec §8 gives only the 576 group total and the 300 trigger. The buttons have 6px and 9px of spare room.
- Item 6: I found no supported override for the other Material-internal selectors (header, controls and content padding, Monday header weight, enabled-day fill, selected-day weight, focus outline, the selected-week band, hiding the label-only row), so they stay. The in-range tokens would need a `DateRange` selection, which would change behaviour the frozen tests cover.

## Next
- The reviewer should re-check 378b7fe, especially the kept casing rule and the use of `_userSelection`.
- The coordinator can merge `redesign-fixes`. The e2e layer should cover how the calendar looks, since those Material-internal selectors remain.</result>

## Findings
No new blockers or majors. Two optional items:

- [Nit] `web/src/styles.scss` (the `.mat-calendar-period-button` lowercase and `::first-letter` rules) — the casing CSS is a workaround for Material's `toLocaleUpperCase()`. That call is at `datepicker.mjs:2076-2077`, and I confirmed it. It works: the screenshot shows "July 2026". It is acceptable, but it depends on the header's internal DOM (`.mdc-button__label &gt; span`), which could break on a Material upgrade. The exact 22.2.0 pin lowers that risk. The alternative is a custom `headerComponent`, which is more code than this needs, so I would not do it.
- [Nit] `web/src/app/features/dashboard/components/week-picker.ts:69` — `_userSelection` is a public output, listed in the `.d.ts` at lines 395 and 816 and in the ɵcmp outputs. It is underscore-prefixed by convention, so Material could rename it without a deprecation cycle. The behaviour is correct: it fires on every user pick, including a re-pick of the current week. `selectedChange` is emitted only when the date differs from the current selection (`datepicker.mjs:2304-2310`). `choose` handles `null`, closes the picker, and emits `weekSelected` only when the week changed. I accept it, and the exact version pin covers the upgrade risk.

## Fix-by-fix check
1. Re-selecting the current week now closes the picker. Confirmed from Material's source and from `choose`, with no regression. The full suite passes, including the user-approved test.

The one-line comment at `styles.scss` meets CLAUDE.md rule 2. It is a single line, and it gives a why that code can't express, because Material upper-cases the label internally. ### 🤖 ASSISTANT — 2026-09-29T02:17:35.081Z

Nothing is broken; this is how the rule works. Each activity-type view is compared only with its own history. Here's Metro Collision Centers, week of Jul 20, Site M, straight from the API:

| View | Site M this week | Usual range | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Appointments set** | 3 | 0–2 | **Higher than usual** (red) |
| Calls received | 2 | 0–6 | Within usual range |
| Leads created | 2 | 0–7 | Within usual range |
| **All activity** | 7 | 1–9 | Within usual range |

**Why "All activity" doesn't flag it:** an appointment count of 3 is unusual for Site M, since it normally gets 0–2. But in "All activity" those appointments are added to calls and leads, which were normal. A combined total moves around more from week to week, so its usual range is wider (1–9). One or two extra appointments inside a total of 7 is within that normal swing.

Each view answers a different question:
- **All activity:** "Is Site M's overall activity normal?" Yes, 7 is ordinary.
- **Appointments set:** "Are Site M's appointments normal?" No, 3 is high for them.

This is the approved rule from PLAN (D2: each view is compared with its own 8-week history). The same holds at account level, where appointments were 19 against a usual 7–21, so still normal.

<result>## Summary
I added a "Picker follow-up" row to the canvas with four new artboards. Three show the picker open on the week of Jun 29 for account 6 (Metro Collision Centers, Leads), one per option, each with the "Latest week" control. The fourth compares three placements for that control. I recommend option C, a week list built on the CDK listbox, with "Latest week" in the picker footer.

## Changed
All in `/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-josemejia-Documents-Software-Development-Qualitara-relay-activity-health/74b25094-c0f6-4b4a-a9ba-66244c7b622d/scratchpad/canvas/project/`:
- `PickerOptionA.dc.html` (new): keep MatCalendar. It opens on June, the Jun 30 band ends square to hint that the week continues, and Jul 1–5 stay blank.
- `PickerOptionB.dc.html` (new): ng-bootstrap `NgbDatepicker` with outside days. A fixed six-week grid, the whole row Jun 29 – Jul 5 shaded, and Jul 6–12 shown as outside days.
- `PickerOptionC.dc.html` (new): a scrollable week list grouped by month, with rows like "Mon Jun 29 – Sun Jul 5". The selected row is focused and marked with a check.
- `LatestWeekPlacement.dc.html` (new): the three placements drawn 1:1 on the approved filter row, plus the footer button enabled and disabled.
- `canvas.json`: added 4 boards at y 10180 (below Spec), the row title `row-picker-follow-up` at y 9880, 2 sticky notes (where the numbers come from, and option C's keyboard), and 4 names at the end of `order`. No existing key or entry changed.
- Scratchpad only: `check_tags.py` (a tag-balance checker, not a deliverable).

## Verification
- `python3 -c "import json; …"` on canvas.json → `13 13 [...9 notes]`. The JSON is valid and boards match order.
- `python3 scratchpad/check_tags.py … PickerOptionA/B/C, LatestWeekPlacement` → all `mismatches [] unclosed []`.
- `ls -la project/`: the existing artboards keep their 02:28 timestamps, so they are untouched.
- Numbers: `curl localhost:5080/api/accounts/6/activity-health?week=2026-06-29&amp;type=lead_created` → summary 15 leads, usually 8–27, `normal`. 15 rows, Site H 3 vs 0–2 `above` first, all others `normal`. Nothing was rendered, published or committed.

## Deviations
- **Where the numbers come from:** PLAN §13 has a golden for account 6, Jun 29 only for `type=all`, not Leads. As the brief asked for Leads, I used the running API's output (same approach as the existing Spike sticky) and recorded it in the `sticky-follow-up-source` note.
- **Trigger name:** the new boards use the approved C-28 order ("{C-17}, choose week"). The old `Picker.dc.html` still has the earlier order; I left it untouched.

## Uncertain
**1. Can Angular Material 22 MatCalendar show days from the neighbouring months? No.**
- In the installed 22.2.0 source (`fesm2022/datepicker.mjs:1115,1179`), `MatMonthView` leaves blank offset cells, then creates cells only for `i &lt; daysInMonth`.
- `MatCalendar`'s only customisation hook is `headerComponent`. `dateClass` only adds a class to days that already exist.
- Both feature requests are open, labelled P4, and have no maintainer reply or linked PR: [#26768](https://github.com/angular/components/issues/26768) and [#29549](https://github.com/angular/components/issues/29549). Related: [#27441](https://github.com/angular/components/issues/27441).

**"Why not show the next month instead?"** Opening July for the week of Jun 29 doesn't work with MatCalendar. The Monday is the only day you can choose and the only place focus can land, and it lives in June. In July's grid the selected week would have no Monday to show, focus or re-choose.

**2. Options**
- **(a) Keep MatCalendar.**
  - For: no change in code or packages, and the approved Spec and UI-48/49 still hold.
  - Against: the user's request is not met. The best it can do is a square band end on Jun 30. The "open on the month holding most of the week" idea doesn't work, for the focus reason above.
- **(b) ng-bootstrap `NgbDatepicker`** ([docs](https://ng-bootstrap.github.io/#/components/datepicker/overview), [releases](https://github.com/ng-bootstrap/ng-bootstrap/releases)).
  - Version: 21.0.0 supports Angular `^22.0.0` (npm, updated 2026-06-22).
  - Fit: it shows neighbouring days by default (`outsideDays: 'visible'`), weeks start on Monday by default, `markDisabled` handles only-Mondays, and `dayTemplate` draws the band. Keyboard support covers arrows, PageUp/PageDown and Home/End.
  - For: it does exactly what the user described.
  - Against:
    - A second component library for one widget, which Spec §7 already rejected for PrimeNG/ngx-bootstrap.
    - It needs extra packages (`@angular/localize`, `@popperjs/core`) and uses its own date type (`NgbDate`) with an adapter.
    - Its styling assumes Bootstrap classes, so we'd restyle it ourselves.
    - Six of every seven cells are still disabled.
    - Known issue with outside days when more than one month is shown: [#2879](https://github.com/ng-bootstrap/ng-bootstrap/issues/2879).
  - Also looked at: PrimeNG 22.1.1 (`showOtherMonths`) has the same second-library cost, but heavier.
- **(c) Week list with the Angular CDK listbox** (`@angular/cdk/listbox`, `cdkListbox`/`cdkOption`; [docs](https://material.angular.dev/cdk/listbox/overview)).
  - For:
    - Every row is a whole week, so crossing months is solved by design.
    - It lists only Mondays from `earliestWeek` to `latestCompleteWeek`, so no row is disabled and Jul 27 never appears.
    - No new package, since CDK is already installed. Keyboard support follows the ARIA listbox pattern: roving focus, Home/End, typeahead, selection only on Enter/Space or click.
    - With the list running oldest to newest, Up from Jul 20 reaches Jul 13, so UI-49's key steps still hold.
  - Against:
    - It no longer looks like a calendar.
    - The list grows by 52 rows a year (26 today for account 6). Month headings and opening scrolled to the selected week reduce that.
    - UI-48 wording ("Tue–Sun cannot be chosen", "whole week shaded") needs rewording by product. Picker tests need rewriting.
    - `@angular/material`, its date-fns adapter and `provideDateFnsAdapter` could be dropped. That is a `frontend`/architect call, and a PLAN §13 amendment.

**3. Recommendation: C.** It meets the request with no new library and the strongest accessibility story. If the user wants to keep a month grid, B is the fallback. Worth checking: does CDK listbox's `@ContentChildren(CdkOption, {descendants: true})` pick up options inside `role="group"` wrappers (for the month headings)? `frontend` should confirm this in a spike.

**4. "Latest week" placement: the picker footer.**
- **Stepper row:** doesn't fit. It needs 132px, but the row has 20px spare (1100 of 1120), so Activity type would be pushed 112px past the content edge, or Spec §8's fixed 576 group would have to change.
- **Beside the "Week" label:** a 44px target would cover 7px of the stepper.
- **Picker footer:** no layout change, and it sits under C-30, so "latest" visibly means Jul 20. The cost is two clicks instead of one.

Behaviour: clicking closes the picker, returns focus to the trigger and emits `weekSelected(latestCompleteWeek)`. It uses native `disabled` when the week is already `latestCompleteWeek`, matching the stepper buttons. There is no API or contract change. Please confirm the two-click trade-off is acceptable.

**5. Copy proposals for product**
- **L-01:** button "Latest week". Its accessible name matches the visible text. Alternatives: "Go to latest week", "Latest complete week".
- **L-02 (C):** listbox accessible name "Weeks". Month headings like "June 2026" come from the dates.
- **L-03 (C):** rows show C-17 without the year ("Mon Jun 29 – Sun Jul 5"). Each option's accessible name is the full C-17 with the year. The typeahead label is like "Jun 29".
- **L-04 (C):** drop C-29 "Weeks run Monday to Sunday." because each row shows it. C-30 stays.
- **L-05 (B):** C-31 "Choose month and year" doesn't exist with ng-bootstrap's arrow navigation. Its "Previous month"/"Next month" match C-31.

## Next
- The coordinator and the user pick A, B or C and approve L-01…L-05.
- If C is chosen:
  - The architect writes a §13 amendment (the picker becomes a CDK listbox, Material datepicker packages possibly removed). Product rewords UI-48/UI-49. `test-author` rewrites the picker red tests.
  - Then `frontend` builds it. The `WeekPicker` inputs and outputs stay the same. Rows come from date-fns `eachWeekOfInterval(earliestWeek, latestCompleteWeek, {weekStartsOn: 1})`, grouped by the Monday's month. It needs `cdkListboxNavigationWrapDisabled`, must open scrolled to the selected row with focus on it, and the popover width stays 350.
- Design details for C, using the existing tokens:
  - Rows: 44px high, radius 8, 15/400 tabular figures.
  - Selected row: `fill-muted` background, weight 600, an ink check icon (`aria-hidden`), and `aria-selected`.
  - Focus: a 2px ink outline, inset 2px.
  - Month heading: 13/600 `ink-2`, 32px high.
  - Scroll area: 438px.</result>

<result>## Verdict: MERGE

## Findings (ranked)
No blockers or majors. Nothing needs a user call.

- [Nit] `week-picker.ts:158-161` — `chooseLatestWeek()` calls `close()` and then `emit()`, which is the same close-then-emit pair as `choose()`. It skips the `!== week().start` guard, which is safe because the native `disabled` attribute stops that click. This is optional and I would not escalate it.
- [Nit] `styles.scss` — the button styling is inline literals (`44px`, `14px`, `8px`). They match the design file exactly and the neighbouring rules use the same style. Leave it.

## Verification
- **Run:** `git checkout --detach 2569b28`, then `npm ci`, `npm test -- --watch=false` and `npm run build`.
- **Tests:** 11 files and 170 tests passed. This includes the UI-50 tests in `week-picker.spec.ts:275` and `dashboard.page.spec.ts:506`.
- **Build:** succeeded.
- **Scope:** the diff touches only `week-picker.ts` (+9) and `styles.scss` (+23). There are no spec, contract, PLAN or docs edits.

## Checks against the brief
- **C-33 and UI-50:** the label is exactly `Latest week`, it is a native `&lt;button type="button"&gt;`, and it sits in a `.footer` div under the `.helper` range line (C-30).
- **Native disabled:** `[disabled]="isLatestWeekSelected()"` is `week().start === latestCompleteWeek()`. The button stays visible when disabled. This matches the PLAN §13 amendment.
- **Never after `latestCompleteWeek`:** the only value emitted is `this.latestCompleteWeek()`. Nothing computes a date, so Mon Jul 27 (the in-progress week) can't be emitted.
- **Focus to trigger:** `close()` runs `trigger.focus()` before the emit. In the disabled-trigger case (`earliestWeek == latestCompleteWeek`) the button is also disabled, so the click can't happen.
- **New history entry:** the emit goes through the existing `weekSelected` output, the same path as `choose()`. There is no contract change.
- **Design (footer variant):**
  - The CSS matches `LatestWeekPlacement.dc.html` lines 98-111 value for value. That covers `margin-top: 14px`, `height: 44px`, `padding: 0 16px`, the border colour `#8c8f95`, the 8px radius and the 14px/500 font.
  - The disabled tokens `#f1f0ec`, `#7c7f85` and `#d6d3cc` match the design's disabled variant.
  - Both screenshots agree with the design: enabled for the Jun 29 week, greyed for Jul 20.
- **CLAUDE.md rules:** no comments, no `new`-ed dependencies, names are domain-based, no magic numbers beyond the design px values, and no client-side statistics.</result>

## Uncertain
**Option A: grouped columns with subheads.** The heading sits on top, with three columns below: "How it's compared" (C-09, C-12, and C-14 when present), "What's counted" (C-10, C-11) and "Freshness" (Data as of).
- *Why:* it answers the user's complaint about missing grouping most directly. Each line now sits under a topic, and C-14 goes into the group it belongs to.
- *Trade-off:* it needs three new copy labels. The Freshness column holds a single line, and when `dataAsOf` is null (UI-22) the whole column, subhead included, has to disappear. The All activity state looks a little sparse.

**Option B: fact strip with icons, caveats below.** Three tiles, each with an icon, for C-09, C-10 and C-11. Below them, a "Keep in mind" list with C-12 and C-14. "Data as of" sits small on the right, level with the heading.
- *Why:* it is the quickest to scan, and the three key facts read as a strip.
- *Trade-off:* it is the heaviest option. Tiles and icons in the footer compete with the page content, which pushes against the "no decoration that carries no meaning" rule. It needs one new label and three new icons in `app-icon` (history, inbox, which is the existing `empty-inbox` path, and copies), plus an info icon. It also makes the footer about 40% taller.

**Option C: freshness tag and ruled notes (recommended).** A left rail holds the heading with a "Data as of" tag under it: 36px high, radius 8, 1px line border, clock icon. On the right, the method lines sit in a two-column list with a thin rule above each line, in today's order, with C-14 last.
- *Why:* it needs no new copy and keeps today's structure (240px rail, two columns), so the frontend change is small. Freshness is clearly separate from the method notes, and the rules turn loose lines into a tidy list.
- *Trade-off:* the lines still aren't grouped by topic. With 5 lines the second column ends one row short. The tag has to stay rectangular with an outline, because a filled pill would look like the "Within usual range" status badge.

**Recommendation:** C. It gives the clearest improvement for the least copy and test risk. If product wants explicit grouping, A is the second choice.

**Copy proposals for product (none are approved yet):**
- Option A: F-03 "How it's compared", F-04 "What's counted", F-05 "Freshness".
- Option B: F-06 "Keep in mind".
- Option C: no new copy.

**Test and acceptance impact:**
- The C-25 spec test (`dashboard.page.spec.ts`) needs two things, and all three options meet both:
  - `About these numbers` must come before every line in the footer's text order.
  - Each line must be one unbroken string.
- For the frontend: "Data as of" must stay after the h2 in the DOM, even though it looks right-aligned in B and sits under the heading in C, and it must stay in one element. The C-26 base line is unchanged.
- UI-06, UI-13, UI-20b, UI-22 and UI-47 check that lines are present, not how the footer is built, so the rows need no change. Option A's subheads add h3 elements, which break no current assertion.
- The Spec's "Type ≠ all" note says C-14 comes "before Data as of". That still holds in DOM order for A and B. In C it holds visually only because "Data as of" moves to the rail, so if C is chosen, that Spec line needs a small wording update.
- Where the rows come from: the Site D row (6, 3–12) is from Main.dc.html. The Site N row (2, 0–6) is from the PickerOptionC API output (account 6, Leads, Jun 29), which has no PLAN golden.

<result>## Summary
The three fact tiles in footer option B now use a soft sage green: a light green fill, a thin deeper-green border, and a green icon on a white disc with a green ring. I did this in both states (All activity, and Leads with C-14). The text stays in the normal dark ink. The "Keep in mind" list and the "Data as of" line stay grey on purpose. All copy is unchanged, and so is the board height. I did not render, publish or commit anything.

New tokens, all at oklch hue 150, set as CSS variables on each `&lt;footer&gt;`:

| Token | Hex | oklch | Contrast |
|---|---|---|---|
| `--color-info-tint` (tile fill) | `#E6F7E9` | 0.960 / 0.026 / 150.8 | Text `#1A1B1E` on it: **15.45:1** |
| `--color-info-border` (tile border, disc ring) | `#B8D8BD` | 0.851 / 0.050 / 149.6 | Decorative edge only: 1.39:1 on the tint, 1.54:1 on white. The fill change marks the tile, not the border. |
| `--color-info-icon` (icon stroke) | `#498D5A` | 0.585 / 0.105 / 150.2 | **4.01:1** on the white disc it sits on; 3.60:1 on the tint. Both are above the 3:1 needed for icons. |

- I did not add a `--color-info-ink`. Keeping the text in the normal ink holds the type hierarchy steady, and green text could start to look like a status. If one is ever needed, oklch(0.40 0.07 150) = `#295233` gives 8.02:1 on the tint.
- **Lightness against the red and blue** (oklch L):

  | Colour | L | Hue |
  |---|---|---|
  | Higher (red `#A8201A`) | 0.476 | 28 |
  | Lower (blue `#173F8A`) | 0.387 | 262 |
  | Green icon | 0.585 | 150 |
  | Green tint | 0.960 | 150 |

  - The icon is 0.11 lighter than the red and 0.20 lighter than the blue. The tint is only used as a background fill, and no status colour is ever a fill.
  - Chroma is 0.105, well below the red (0.172) and the blue (0.133), so the green reads quieter than either status colour.
  - Hue 150 is a warm-leaning sage, so it sits well on the warm off-white ground (hue 91) and doesn't turn minty.
- **Why it won't read as a status:** the tiles keep their large rounded-rectangle shape, sit in the footer, carry descriptive icons, and have no ▲/▼ and no pill shape. The only status colours are the red and blue, and "Within usual range" stays neutral grey.
- **Why "Keep in mind" and "Data as of" stay grey:**
  - The "Keep in mind" items are cautions. Green would suggest reassurance and blur the difference between facts and caveats.
  - "Data as of" is metadata. Keeping the green to one area (the facts) is what makes it stand out, and a second green area would weaken that.

## Changed
- `/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-josemejia-Documents-Software-Development-Qualitara-relay-activity-health/74b25094-c0f6-4b4a-a9ba-66244c7b622d/scratchpad/canvas/project/FooterOptionB.dc.html`: the three tokens added to both `&lt;footer&gt;` styles, plus the fill, border, disc ring and icon stroke on all six tiles. Nothing else changed.
- `canvas.json` did **not** change. The board stays 1440×1160: the 1px border sits inside the tiles' existing 76px minimum height.
- A scratch script for the colour maths, not part of the canvas: `.../scratchpad/ok.py`.

## Verification
- A replacement script found the expected number of matches: 2 footers, 6 tile fills, 6 disc rings, 6 icons.
- `grep -c 'style="stroke: var(--color-info-icon);"'` → 6, and `grep -n 'stroke="var'` → nothing, so no `var()` is left inside an SVG attribute.
- The contrast ratios come from WCAG relative luminance, and the oklch values from Ottosson's sRGB↔OKLab conversion (`ok.py`). Nothing was rendered, as instructed.

## Deviations
None.

## Uncertain
- **For `frontend` to check:** the icon colour is set as `style="stroke: var(--color-info-icon)"` rather than the SVG `stroke=` attribute, because `var()` doesn't work reliably inside SVG attributes. `frontend` should do the same, or use the hex value directly.
- **Design choice for the user:** the tile text stays in the normal ink rather than dark green. If the user wants more green, the fallback `#295233` (8.02:1) is ready.
- **Design choice for the user:** the border fails 3:1 on purpose because it's decorative. If the user reads that as an accessibility gap, darkening it to about oklch 0.72 would pass 3:1 against white.

Tests pass: 11 files, 175 tests. `npm run build` completes with no warnings. The diff touches only `dashboard.page.ts`, `styles.scss` and `angular.json`. It has no test, contract or `icon.ts` edits, and `IconName` already had the four new names.

## Findings (most severe first)

- **[Minor, remove] `web/src/app/features/dashboard/dashboard.page.ts` `@media (max-width: 760px)` block (deviation a)**
  - PLAN §13 says "desktop only (no breakpoints)", and `docs/design/README.md` records the user's approval of "desktop only".
  - Neither `FooterOptionB.dc.html` nor `Piece-iynb.dc.html` has this rule, and no test covers it.
  - It is a breakpoint nobody asked for. It is also the only one in the page, so the rest of the layout is not responsive and the rule buys nothing.
  - Fix: delete the 4-line block. No user call is needed, because the user's own instruction and PLAN both settle it.

- **[Minor, no user call] `web/angular.json` `anyComponentStyle` `maximumWarning` raised from 4kB to 6kB (deviation b)**
  - I accept this. PLAN §13 requires the footer to stay in `DashboardPage`, so extracting a component would contradict the amendment.
  - The `styles` template in `dashboard.page.ts` grew from 4,365 to 6,008 raw bytes.
  - The budget is global, so it also loosens the check for every other component. The 8kB error limit is unchanged.
  - The raw size is now close to the new limit. Log it as debt: extract the footer styles into a shared stylesheet, or into a child component if PLAN is later amended.
  - If the user wants the budget kept tight, it becomes a PLAN amendment. That is optional, not required for merge.

## Deviations from the checklist
None found. Specifically:
- **Footer contents:**
  - C-25 is the `h2`, with the data-as-of tag on the right of the heading row.
  - C-09, C-10 and C-11 are the three fact tiles with the `history`, `empty-inbox` and `copies` icons.
  - `Keep in mind` (C-34) is a real `&lt;h3&gt;`. It holds C-12, and C-14 as the second item only when the type is not `all`.
  - C-26 is unchanged and always shown.
  - The wording is verbatim.
- **Data as of:** the tag is hidden when `dataAsOfLabel` is null (UI-22), and the heading row then holds only the `h2`.
- **Design match:** the CSS matches the `FooterOptionB.dc.html` inline styles value for value: paddings 40/24, tile 76px min-height with a 12px radius, icon circle 40px, and `Keep in mind` at 120px, 13px and ink-3. It also matches the README tag spec: white background, `--color-line` border, 8px radius, 5px 12px padding, 32px min-height, tabular numbers.
- **Tokens:** the three info tokens match the PLAN values (`#E6F7E9`, `#B8D8BD`, `#498D5A`). They are used only on the fact tiles (background and border, plus the icon circle's border and stroke). The data-as-of tag is neutral and there is no green anywhere else. Nothing uses the tokens for status.
- **Icons:** `app-icon` sets `aria-hidden="true"` on its host, and the template has no text. Every use in the footer goes through it. The tag's text content is exactly "Data as of …".
- **Contrast:**
  - Tile text `#1A1B1E` on `#E6F7E9` is about 15:1.
  - The green icon stroke `#498D5A` on white is about 4.0:1 by my calculation. That clears the 3:1 non-text minimum, and the icons are decorative anyway.
  - `Keep in mind` in ink-3 `#6A6D73` on white is about 5:1.
  - The list text in ink-2 is higher than that.
- **Code rules:** no comments and no XML doc blocks. Names are domain-based. No new behaviour classes, and no client-side statistics.
- **Screenshots:** `footer-all.png` and `footer-leads.png` match the design, including the two-item `Keep in mind` list with C-14 in the Leads state.

## Clean areas
- Contract and tests: clean.
- Scope aside from the media query: clean.
- URL state, API and SQL: not touched by this diff.

None. The media query goes because of the user's "desktop only" instruction. I recommend accepting the budget raise and noting it in the AI log.</result>

<result>## Summary
I audited the deliverables that PLAN and the brief require, then updated the README and the product-owned docs for session 3. Everything is in one commit, `ce8c04e`, on this worktree's branch, based on `ca98e98`. Two gaps are left, and neither is mine to fix: AI_LOG.md has no session-3 entries and its Reflection and Tools/models sections are still empty. The e2e deferral also has no PLAN §13 entry yet.

## Changed
- `README.md` (full rewrite of the product sections, plus the running and UI sections)
- `docs/decisions.md`:
  - three redesign rows added to "Later decisions";
  - the "E2E planned" wording removed;
  - three deferred rows added: e2e with `e2e-scaffold`, adjacent-month days in the picker, desktop only and no pagination;
  - "another day" reprioritised to include the per-type hint.
- `docs/interpretation.md`: "footnote" becomes "page footer"; the empty-account row now says the whole week control is disabled; a limit line added for "All activity" hiding a single-type change.
- `docs/running.md`: the `dev.sh` port checks and its "Open" message; "What you should see" now covers the picker bounds and the footer.
- `docs/acceptance-criteria.md`: the three stale "no §13 entry yet" citations now point to the §13 redesign entry and its amendments.

## Verification
- Every file listed above was edited with an assert-once replacement script, so each target string matched exactly one place. `git diff --stat` showed 5 files, +133/−20.
- I checked the new UI claims against the code:
  - the route redirects `''` to `dashboard`;
  - the template has `Customer admin`, `About these numbers`, `Keep in mind` and `Relay · Activity health`;
  - the ◀/▶ stepper is still in `dashboard-filters.ts`;
  - `package.json` on main has no `e2e` script.
- The `e2e-scaffold` claims come from `git show e2e-scaffold:web/playwright.config.ts`: `webServer` runs only `npm start`, with the comment "The API is not started here".
- The `designer` agent exists in `.claude/agents/`.
- Test counts: running.md, interpretation.md and testing.md contain none, so nothing there was stale. 173 backend comes from PLAN §13 "Test suite pruned" and the session-3 kickoff. I did not verify 175 web; it is the coordinator's figure, and I didn't run anything.

## Deviations
- The README now repeats short versions of material that lives in docs/interpretation.md and docs/decisions.md. The brief asks for these sections in the README, and PLAN §13 says the README itself must carry the known limits verbatim. The docs remain the detailed source.
- I added "Mobile layout, pagination" as a deferred item with the reason "at most 15 locations per account" (from PLAN §2). The scope comes from PLAN §13; the wording is mine.

## Uncertain
- **The "All activity" limit has no number behind it.** It rests on the user decision the coordinator passed on. I wrote it without figures; an `analysis/` query would make it evidenced.
- **PLAN.md is out of date on e2e (possible PLAN error).** §13 "UI/UX redesign → Order" still says the Playwright specs are written after the redesign lands. No entry records the 2026-09-29 deferral or the scaffold's API-must-already-run deviation. Someone other than me needs to add a §13 entry.
- **Product sign-off on new copy.** §13 says the redesign's new copy waits for `product`'s approval. The acceptance criteria already record C-24…C-34 as accepted, so I treated that approval as given.

## Next

It should say deferred by user decision (2026-09-29), point to the `e2e-scaffold` branch, and note that the API must already be running. The frontend row could mention the week picker tests.
- **coordinator, `AI_LOG.md`:**

  - write the Reflection and Tools/models sections;
  - re-run `scripts/export-ai-log.sh` and check that both leak counts are 0.
- **coordinator, PLAN §13:** add the e2e deferral entry described under Uncertain.
- **Debt:** none added. I left the component style budget out, as instructed.</result>

I applied the cleanup round to the session-3 web code in my worktree. All 175 tests pass, the build has no warnings, and all six screenshot views came out pixel- and byte-identical to the baseline. Fixes 1 to 8 are in. Fix 9 is skipped because the page's styles are still over 4 kB.
